<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Considering the protein&#x27;s role in DNA topology, which cellular component is the best fit for its GO cellular-component assignment?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Chromosome<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Cytosol<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Plasma membrane<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Extracellular region<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence contains several hallmark motifs of Type IA topoisomerases:<br>- The &quot;YGD&quot; motif (seen in &quot;YGDRESE&quot;) is characteristic of the TOPRIM domain found in topoisomerases<br>- The catalytic serine/tyrosine region (&quot;GATMFGPIEITEQTK&quot;) is conserved in topoisomerase III-like enzymes<br>- The &quot;ETGG&quot; and &quot;DGDPWS&quot; motifs are conserved regions of the topoisomerase catalytic core<br><br>The question explicitly states this protein&#x27;s role is in &quot;DNA topology,&quot; confirming it is a topoisomerase. Topoisomerases regulate DNA supercoiling, resolve tangles, and manage DNA topology during replication, transcription, and recombination.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- **A. Chromosome**: Topoisomerases physically associate with chromosomal DNA to regulate its topology.
